ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e2100	In the course of autonomy, characteristic values have been drawn up. In the course of the dedicated conduct, the school was given the opportunity to spend the revenue it has for its own needs, for its own needs. In other words, a school can generate revenue within the framework of this dedicated gesture for premises it rents. There is also the possibility of partial jurisdiction, which has also been re-adopted by Parliament. This allows schools to sell offers, knowledge offers, courses and special courses on the adult education market. This can also generate revenue for schools.

I think that's something very essential. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Oh, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no,]] The Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats will certainly be right for me to say that the referendum is an important part of direct democracy. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] [[Abg. Dr. Schwimmer: This is a minority stand, Mr. Krüger!]] Dear Members of the Austrian People's Party! One can, of course, resort to the superlative point of view and say: What's the citizen's concern? What does the citizen care about me? Once he has given his vote in the election cell, we forget him for four years, and shortly before the next National Council election, we'll pack out our advertising and election slogans again. - That's obviously your attitude. Therefore, if we request a referendum on such an important issue as the question of schilling and the introduction of the euro. Ladies and gentlemen! A poorer concept of democracy, a less valued attitude to the essence of direct democracy, to the direct will of the citizen than you unfortunately show it, can't be found otherwise! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] However, ladies and gentlemen, it is not only the question of whether, in such a important matter, a referendum, a decision by the Sovereign, by the citizens of this country should be made voluntarily, but even the question of whether a referendum should not be adopted in accordance with constitutional considerations. You know that the Austrian federal constitution then imperatively requires a referendum if essential elements of our constitution are to be amended or put out of force. And, ladies and gentlemen, who will argue that the Austrian Schilling is an important part of the overall structure of our constitution?- Oh, my God! The Austrian schilling occurs in countless laws, including laws that are in constitutional order. It is not only a question of the basic concept of democracy, of the basic understanding of the governmental parties towards the sovereign, to the direct democracy, but also of the rule of law and the law of the government to carry out this referendum. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Ladies and gentlemen! The propaganda machine that the government has launched on the occasion of this popular desire for "shilling-population" has not only reminded me, but, I believe, also very many other, particularly large parts of the population, of the immense propaganda machine on the occasion of the preparation for EU accession. At that time, no argument was too cheap to be thrown into the scales to deceive voters on the impact of accession to the European Union. At the time, it was said that pensions were at risk of an above-average rate of inflation if Austria did not join the European Union. That the schilling is not lost, but that it is maintained: it was also one of those one-sided propaganda tales that you have tried to show the Austrians - and unfortunately you have partly succeeded. For there were a number of speeches by prominent Members and politicians of the Government who, before the referendum on accession to the European Union, had promised high and holy that accession to the European Union would not change the status of schilling. And what happened in practice? - We were scarcely at the European Union, this line of argument was left, and once again, on the occasion of this schilling-popular desire, the propaganda machine was launched, namely for millionates, and with all possible true and false stories and fairy tales, they have tried to prevent the Austrians from actually signing this popular desire. Ladies and gentlemen! The introduction of the euro is not only about giving up our favorite schilling, but also about the expectations associated with the euro. And it is also a question of whether the introduction of a single currency should be in Europe at the beginning or in the first third of an integration process, or whether it should be the coronation. For one thing the past has shown, over the last 2,000 years, that there has never been a large economic area in the history of civilized humanity, to which artificially a common currency has been required. And that's the jumping point! My group is not against the euro, but we believe that the introduction of the euro is premature, because integration has not yet progressed so far. Although it is said that the convergence criteria have been met by the candidate countries, what is the practical situation? How did these convergence criteria come about? Have they actually been implemented by the individual economies? I mean, one cannot rightly answer that all the criteria have been met. Yes Believe You really believe that, for example, Portugal has the stability that would actually allow the country to participate in the euro, in the European monetary union? Or do you think that the various budget tricks, which our Minister of Finance is also serving, actually guarantee that the convergence criteria are not only nominal but also economically respected? We do not believe this, and it therefore raises the question: Has this Maastricht Treaty been fulfilled at all? - Professor Adamovich, President of the Constitutional Court, said that this question is of course constitutional, because if a Treaty is concluded - as is the Maastricht Treaty, which is also part of the Treaty of Accession of Austria - then it must be checked whether the conditions there were actually complied with or not. If one comes to the conclusion that they have not been respected, for example because Portugal does not meet the convergence criteria, because Belgium does not meet the convergence criteria, because Austria fulfils them only because certain budget tricks, certain budget cosmetics, have been applied, then the question arises whether this Treaty has been respected at all. So if one comes to this conclusion - and renowned scientists share this opinion - then one must say that the convergence criteria and thus the conditions for an economic union have not been met. Ladies and gentlemen! A short while ago, on the citizens' initiative to repeat the EU referendum. In the Committee, there has been clarity about the fact that the EU referendum cannot be repeated. I think it is important for us to confess to this; this is also happening from the side of our group. One cannot repeat such a referendum just a short time later, if one has once presented such an important question to the Sovereign and after having been supported by almost two thirds of the accession. It is not possible to repeat votes until a decent outcome comes out. One has to submit to the Sovereign, and this Sovereign has just decided to join the European Union.

I am pleased, for example, that we have succeeded in reaching an amendment to the Banking Act that there is no discrimination on the part of the local authorities against the other local authorities. All in all, it can be assumed that the Euro-Funding Accompaniment Act is once again one of the steps we are taking to achieve this changeover in a correct, serious, well-prepared manner. It is a great change, it is a change which, of course, is also linked to considerable costs, which are also beneficial, but it is, I believe, a change that is in the interests of the entire Austrian economy, which contributes to greater stability within this large currency area and which we therefore welcome.

We Liberals will agree because we believe that we must continue this process of adjustment, because, Mr Federal Minister - I say quite openly - we have confidence in the top officials of your home that they are doing these adaptation laws in a technical and factual manner. The difficulty of the levels is ahead of us, many, many adjustments and changes. Whoever knows the Austrian soul knows that the word "new" is always understood as a threat because it is associated with change. So there will be some changes. I call on you, Mr Federal Minister, to submit a proposal to the House as soon as possible, not only after many years, which shows how your idea of the Austrian National Bank should redefine its tasks. There is a need to discuss the question of minimum reserves, the question of reserves, the question of the scope of the task. This is not a debate that needs to be held here and today, but I would ask you not to hold this debate in camera caritatis, but to take this discussion into the financial committee of the House in order to let the House work there. There is no doubt that the changeover to the farms will cost money. There will be a lot of trouble, especially with a currency awarding law. This is as necessary as a crook. In the time of the information society, in the time when a pocket calculator with euro conversion - we've been working on it - it's much easier to have every entrepreneur in Austria, from the sausage stand to the Billa, from the micro-enterprise to the large group, to provide their customers with a pocket calculator for free, where you only need to type in the numbers and press a schilling or Eurotasse. Even if someone should take one or two or three computers, he will not take more than ten, for what should people do with more computers? It still comes a lot, much cheaper, if the economy spends 100, 200, 300 or even 400 million schilling on it, providing people with a total information - modern electronic - than if we need to change all computer programs for half a year or three quarters of a year or write new price lists for half or three quarters of a year. The Economic Research Institute speaks of costs of 1 to 2 billion schilling. Instead of spending this money, we would rather pass on a new advertising medium, which the major branded product manufacturers will deliver according to their needs, so that there is a conversion from euro to Schilling everywhere. That is how we save ourselves from this currency changeover law. I think it is a nonsense to discuss it at all. The learning process in the population is much more supported by this calculator and other small aids on which you can set the currency conversion by turning. From 2002, at the latest in June, perhaps in October, the euro will be history, it will be natural. Until then, the Presidency of Austria is also over in the European Union, which, from an economic point of view, must, from a liberal point of view, bring about the further development of the European partnership. Our export industry benefits from this every day. The export boom can be traced back to it. However, the elimination of domestic discrimination, which we still have in Austria in many, many areas, would be an internal task for the Austrian Presidency, a task which we must do internally. We still have a commercial right, which discriminates against domestics in commercial access. We have a shop closure that discriminates against domestic businesses. We have a bureaucracy burden that causes costs and discriminates against Austrian companies. Our labour costs are the fourth highest in the European Union, our gross wages are only the 9th highest in the European Union, and the tax law in the indirect field - think of the much quoted drink tax, I have been allowed to present it to the Minister of Finance often - will result in people in the euro area buying their beer where it is 15 percent cheaper. Because for 15 percent savings per box of beer, Mr. Finanzminister, you like to cross the Innbrücke to Simbach. [[Federal Minister Edlinger: But not from Vienna!]] Not from Vienna, but from Braunau! In January 1999, at least for 11 Member States, i.e. by means of the common currency and by the abolition of exchange rate fluctuations which the Austrian economy, for example, cost about one percent of the gross domestic product in 1995. Negotiating this package is the question of the next three-quarters of a year, and Austria has a very clear programme of how this will be done. It is not the hope that we will be able to resolve Agenda 2000 from the financial point of view until December, but it is about creating the conditions in the technical field, in the field of horizontal flows of funds, but of course also relatively far up to the possibility of political decisions. Anyone who has once negotiated internal Austrian financial compensation knows that, of course, until the last possible moment, the respective positions will be represented in the subjective interest of the respective State. That is why we have to start these negotiations - and I say this clearly - with rest. You can't get nervous. Whoever becomes nervous - I know this from internal Austrian financial compensatory negotiations - is depositing money. That's not a question at all. Of course, it is theoretically possible to implement tax harmonisation first, then to carry out a coordination of social standards, of environmental standards, and to apply only at the end of the currency. But because of the normative power of the facsimile, i.e. by the fact that everyone in the European Union is convinced to have the optimal tax system, the optimal social system, an environmental standard system suitable for the benefit of citizens, nothing happens except greetings, unless one creates framework conditions by which pressure is exerted on these differences - as a second and third step in order to eliminate those irritations that I have spoken of -. To that end, I believe that economic and monetary union in these eleven states will exert pressure on the harmonisation of tax and social systems and on environmental standards, but not in the sense of a levelling down, because there are democratic societies in all these countries, and this must also take account of social and environmental standards. In this respect, Mr Dr Krüger, I believe that the common European currency is the one that we need in order to create the European framework for the best possible response to international challenges in the interests of the citizens of our countries.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e11165	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The following comment on the first report, namely the agreement with the Slovak Republic on cooperation and mutual assistance in the event of disasters. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that this agreement is a very important one for our country and must also be seen in the light of the fact that the Federal Government has not succeeded in preventing the start-up of a nuclear power plant in our neighbouring country of Slovakia. It is absolutely crucial that, at least in the case of cases - and I hope that this will never happen - there is a smooth cooperation and a corresponding mutual support and assistance. However, it is not only about this one possible occasion, but also about the need to provide general assistance in the event of disasters and to do so. I therefore believe that this international cooperation must be strengthened and intensified. For this reason, this Treaty is of great importance and importance. It regulates the prevention and control of disasters, the definition of contact points and provides for the facilitation of border crossing and, most importantly, the facilitation of imports and exports of related supplies. It is important to help quickly, effectively and efficiently in the event of a disaster. However, it is also about the regulation of claims. I think it is necessary to act unbureaucratically. It also makes no sense and does not benefit from counting on each other the costs incurred. Above all, it is important that those helpers involved in international disaster aid are held to be harmful for any damage that may occur in the event of such assistance. I therefore believe that this agreement is of great importance to our country. However, it is also important that we take account of our country's clear responsibilities and clear competences. We must take the necessary measures and steps in the context of civil defence and in the context of Austrian civil protection in order to ensure that coordinated cooperation with the relief forces abroad and their own forces operating in the event of disaster aid can function without complaint. Federal Minister! I believe that we need to rethink in this area and that there is a need for action. It will be necessary to achieve more efficient and enhanced coordination in the context of civil defence and civil protection organisations. Furthermore, it will be necessary to coordinate the organisational structures and build up the corresponding communications. In order to be able to provide rapid assistance, the necessary telecommunications connections and the necessary contact points are needed, i.e. the necessary structures. I miss that in our country. I expect the necessary steps to be taken in this regard. [[Congratulations at the Liberal Forum.]] Ladies and gentlemen! It is also important to ensure that the training of auxiliary workers, in particular joint training, is progressed accordingly. I therefore expect the Ministry of the Interior to carry out exercises in which Austrian assistants with Slovak assistants practice and train the disaster aid accordingly in order to achieve optimum cooperation. We will therefore give our approval to this agreement. Ladies and gentlemen! We will not vote in favour of the push agreement with the Republic of Croatia. It is clear that the challenges of the future require increased international cooperation. It will be necessary to work more closely together in the area of justice and police. In fact, there must be the necessary agreements, especially in the field of security management. But these agreements, which can and should also regulate the transfer of persons, must not be at the expense of fundamental fundamental rights and freedoms. I think we are not yet ready to conclude such a move agreement with the Republic of Croatia. We believe that the conditions are not yet met, especially with regard to asylum law. From our point of view, it is important to demand a very high standard so that we can continue to meet our humanitarian obligations in the future. I believe that these conditions are not yet met. It is particularly important that the further removal of a possible illegal that has entered Austria via Croatia could be carried out to the third country, its home country. I believe that this would contradict our human rights principles. That is why we do not want and will not give our approval to this bill, this suspension agreement. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e12936	My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The two agenda items which are now under negotiation are, on the one hand, an agreement on mutual assistance, in this case between two neighbouring countries, Slovakia and Austria. I do not want to go into this now, because my colleague Dr. Moser will do so afterwards. It's about something that's a very critical question at the moment - Tag Mochovce. I would like to focus briefly on the second item on the agenda, which is before you for the vote, regarding the so-called'swimming agreement' - that is not to say, it is just a short formula - between Austria and Croatia. The interesting thing about this is not that it is a push-off agreement - we have often negotiated here - but that it is a push-off agreement which is not concluded with a neighbouring country, not with a country with which Austria has a common border. In principle, I have absolutely nothing against an agreement to allow the deportation of persons who have the citizenship of the respective country with which the agreement is concluded. In my view, this is urgently necessary and therefore desirable. In this context, there are certain formalities that need to be regulated. That's the essence of push agreements. Ladies and gentlemen! However, I am lacking in the understanding of the move agreement which is now under way, in which Croatia, that is, no neighbouring country of Austria, is obliged to take over a third-country foreigner, who at the moment - of course unlawfully - would not try to get him out of the country - is in Austria and has at some point stayed in Croatia. Because that is the subject of this suspension agreement. Push agreements concluded with directly neighbouring countries have a certain logic. For example, someone who, as we had just recently, comes from Hungary illegally to Austria and stops here can be relocated to Hungary. But if someone is to be moved to a country with which we do not have a common border, the question arises, how did he come to Austria? These are people who do not have Croatian citizenship - that is the point of the whole thing. In my opinion, this matter is a serious suspicion that Austria - and in this case the Austrian asylum authorities - really wants to make the so-called safe third country policy we operate "waterproof". For this agreement means that politically persecuted persons, i.e. refugees whose escape route through Croatia has led to Austria, can be sent back to Croatia sometime. And there is a danger that a political refugee in a state like Croatia, which I would certainly not regard as a safe third country - you all know the fragile political situation in Croatia - the right to make an asylum application under the Geneva Convention at all through the deportation from Austria. I therefore believe that this push-off agreement is a fresh proof of Austria's policy to stop against the non-EU neighbouring countries, to make the borders even closer than they already are, and to make it completely impossible for politically, racially or religiously persecuted people on the land. Today, practically every route to Austria leads to a so-called "safe third country" - according to the interpretation of the Ministry of the Interior or the Federal Asylamt. That is why we for the first time reject a push agreement, because the danger of a political persecutor being exposed to a new threat is too great for me and my colleagues in the specific case. As long as not to a high degree There is certainty that refugees also have the opportunity in Croatia to explain their reasons for fleeing in a way that will allow them to do so, so long as we do not agree to push agreements with countries such as Croatia. I would like to mention a second aspect, regardless of that. It is not to be seen that Austria is trying to remove responsibility and hence also burdens on Croatia, a country which is economically substantially poor and is still marked by the effects of the war a few years ago. Slovenia has obviously refused to carry out the Austrian deportation policy. That is why there is now a push agreement with Croatia. I do not know how this has been done, but I can imagine that this has happened not only with political gentle pressure, but also with political necessity. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: Mr. Minister! What did you do?]] [[Abg. Young: You said we're not going on vacation!]] That is the reason for our scepticism and the rejection of this suspension agreement.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e12985	The next speaker is Mr Puttinger. The clock is set to 6 minutes. I'll give him the word.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e13000	Mr President. Mr. Minister! High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! I think Mrs Stoisits has said that we have more in common on the issue of Austria and Slovakia than in the previous discussion which we also had to follow. I would like to address the mutual disaster aid with Slovakia today. As in the case of Austria, as in the whole of Europe, efforts have been made with neighbouring countries to regulate assistance in the event of disasters. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Austria has already concluded contracts with Germany and Liechtenstein in this regard. The contracts with Hungary and Slovenia are immediately in front of the ratification. Contract negotiations are conducted with Italy, Switzerland and the Czech Republic. - There has been no regulation with Slovakia in this area so far. The purpose of this Treaty is to create a international framework for mutual assistance in the event of accidents. High house! This is an extremely important step towards efficient and rapid assistance in the event of disasters. This is because it will create clarity and legal certainty for the use of disasters. I would like to draw from the 18 points of the agreement five or six which, as I believe, are very essential and which should be clarified. First: Of great importance is certainly the clear determination of what Austrian and which Slovak authorities are responsible for receiving aid applications. This is essential and essential for the coordination of accidents. On the Austrian side, the Ministry of the Interior or the State of Lower Austria or the Government of the State of Burgenland will be responsible in case of need, on the Slovak side always the Minister of the Interior. Secondly, Auxiliary teams should be able to pass the respective state borders unbureaucratic. Travel documents are no longer necessary. - The elimination of entry formalities is an extremely important pass. This only enables quick help. You can certainly imagine that valuable time would pass if only the completion of customs and border formalities had to be completed at the border. Thirdly, The use of aircraft for rapid relief measures, especially for the rapid launch of auxiliary teams, is also governed by this Treaty. In the event of a disaster, aircraft can fly to any airport, they only have to report it to air traffic control. Of course, military aircraft have an application to make to the State seeking aid. Fourth: However, the rules on imports of addictions and psychotropic substances into the aid-seekers state are also important. It is stated in the agreement that this is, of course, only permitted in medical terms and that appropriate supervision is also necessary in the form of doctors. Abuse is therefore eliminated from the outset. As a fifth point, I should like to mention the regulation with regard to replacement costs. A State providing assistance does not receive a replacement of the costs of assistance. Sixteenths: Article 2 regulates the claims for damages in Austria and Slovakia. Both states agree to waive claims for damages, whether they are damage to health or to the death of a helper or to property damage. There is no liability framework. - Regrett claims to the aiding State are therefore not raised. It would certainly not be useful or useful to see that if damage were caused in the context of assistance, the aiding State would still be held liable for it. High house! In my view, a very important point in this Treaty is the fact that these benefits are in principle voluntary. Any party is thus free to respond to the request of the competent authority either positively or negatively. Any legal entity in Austria may be free of charge and may decide in the case in question whether or not to provide assistance. Therefore, the Federal Minister for the Interior and the competent state government will only be able to respond to a request for assistance from the Slovak Republic if the most important agencies, whether it be the Red Cross, the auxiliary agency, the labourer Samaritan Federation or the fire brigades, self-appointedly determine the willingness to do so. Thus, there is no legal possibility directly for Austrian authorities to commit legal entities to aid operations under this Treaty. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that this international treaty is an important element in the relations between these two states. This Treaty not only guarantees rapid and unbureaucratic assistance in the event of disasters, but can already be put in place in the preventive cooperation many confidence-building measures agreed for the benefit of Slovakia and Austria. High house! I would therefore ask you to agree to this international agreement.

Both are well justified in the context of this disaster relief agreement, because both are practically essential, for one time not only of the neighbourhood aid, but also of disaster prevention. But you have to think beyond that. In the present case, our agreement applies to this basic condition, the one time the neighbourhood aid in the event of disasters. However, it is necessary in other areas to go further, including with regard to international treaties and trust elements. On the subject of international treaties: At European level - and I see this in particular as a task for the Austrian Presidency - it must in any case come to international agreements on nuclear safety. It is still stated on the national issue that a state will put a nuclear power plant into operation or that it will operate an old one which will be a ticking time bomb not only for its own country, but also for the neighbouring countries, even for the whole of Central Europe. It can't go on that way! Finally, it must be regulated that security standards become mandatory under international law. I see today's decision as a simple exercise of duty and, at the same time, the task of forcing and offensively addressing the essential question of security, namely those in the nuclear field. - So much for the international law! It must finally be started at EU level and put to the best possible conclusion. Now on the point of "confidence building": In my opinion, it was not particularly confident, particularly in connection with the Mochovce nuclear power station, that it was relatively late in 1995, that it was only in 1995 that offers to the Slovak government or the power plant founders that were made to go a different way than that of nuclear energy supply. It would have taken massive steps earlier or at least during the period between 1995 and 1997 to generate confidence in another technology. One could have spread confidence in the technology of the gas steam combination, which could satisfy the energy needs of the Slovak population on a better path than by the construction of Mochovce or by the re-operating of Bohunice. In my opinion, this item on the agenda today, the disaster aid, is definitely a massive call to the Federal Government to do everything possible not to activate reactor blocks 2 and 3 in Mochovce, and - even more importantly - finally to insist that - as Me#iar has promised - Bohunice be shut down. These are the real security tasks which must be addressed in any case in the light of the disaster relief law now in place.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e13226	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Austria and its Western neighbours as well as some other Western European countries have been subject to contractual arrangements for the acquisition of persons at the border for many years. Austria has also been trying for many years to conclude agreements on the acquisition of persons with its other neighbours and with all other Central and Eastern European countries. Now it has succeeded in concluding such an agreement with Croatia, an agreement which my group welcomes. It represents the first push agreement providing for the withdrawal of third countries which have been concluded with a State with which Austria does not link a common border. Why is this agreement with Croatia so important? - To date, no contractual arrangement exists between Austria and Croatia for the acquisition of its own citizens and from illegal third-country nationals and the transit of third-country nationals to the rest of the two countries. However, as such an agreement is of great importance to Austria in the context of a responsible security policy, it is welcome that a contractual arrangement has now been established with the Republic of Croatia. In the EU too, there is a Council recommendation to conclude bilateral readmission agreements between Member States and third countries. Many EU countries have already followed this recommendation, and there is a multilateral agreement between the Schengen States and Poland. The Treaty with Croatia, which is now in place, represents a modern, simple-to-use, EU-pattern push agreement. In this way, corresponding agreements with Hungary, Slovenia and Slovakia were also concluded under this scheme. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, it must also be mentioned in the context of this move agreement with Croatia that one or another can express doubts about Croatia's rule of law, as has already been done in the Committee. I would like to point out that Croatia is a member of the Council of Europe, and it is a commitment to the rule of law. Furthermore, I believe that we should all contribute to the fact that western fundamental values in this young state are also taking place in Croatia. I can imagine that such a contract, as it is available to us today for decision-making, can also contribute to it. Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, I would like to point out that the transfer of third countries from Austria can, of course, be refused if the third country of destination or in any other transit State is at risk of being subjected to inhumane treatment, punishment or even death penalty, or in its life, freedom - for reasons of its race, its religion, nationality, its belonging to a particular social group or because of its social views. Furthermore, it may be rejected if the third-country foreigner in the requested State has to be prosecuted criminally, or if he is threatened by criminal proceedings in the target State or by any other State of transit. The Socialist Group will vote in favour of this agreement.

The aim is to ensure humane treatment according to human rights, apart from the restriction of illegal immigration. As has already been stressed, there is no common border between Austria and Croatia, special commitments are necessary - I emphasise - both parties, and these have been defined. To mention only briefly the three most important points. First: Any formless takeover of its own citizens must be guaranteed. Secondly, A takeover from third countries is mandatory. Thirdly, the transfer of third countries is hereby fixed. In addition to a cost savings envisaged, the rule of readmission is always referred to, of formalities kept as low as possible, and of the reduction of bureaucracy. Each State has also undertaken to recognise, of course, basic international rules and to show responsibility to the persons concerned. In this context, it must also be mentioned that dealing with the laws of foreigners, with the people concerned, is of importance: If life, if a person's freedom is in danger - for example, for reasons of race, religion, political belonging or for the threat of death - then of course special precautions must be taken and removed from a repatriation. Of course, consideration should also be given to sick, old or in need of care. Ladies and gentlemen! This is an agreement that can be approved, in terms of the security of our country and Europe as a whole.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e14461	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Under item 10 of the agenda, we are dealing with the Convention on the Customs Information System (CIS) in 952 of the Supplements. This Convention on the Use of Information Technology in Customs was drawn up in the framework of cooperation in the field of Justice and the Interior of the EU. This Agreement was approved by the European Council in Cannes in June 1995 and adopted on 26 June 1995. It was signed by the ambassadors in June 1995. This Agreement will allow customs administrations to take a common approach and to automatically exchange personal and other data. This Convention is a counterpart to Regulation No. 515, the so-called State Aid Regulation, which entered into force on 13 June. ==References====External links== The purpose of this Regulation is to ensure the proper application of customs and agricultural arrangements on the basis of automated data exchange. Ladies and gentlemen! The purpose of this system is to prevent, investigate and prosecute serious violations of national legislation. This will make it much easier to counter economic crime in Europe. The marketing of prohibited goods subject to restrictions or subject to separate control will be substantially hampered. But the so-called famous money laundering and drug trafficking will certainly be better combated. The Convention lays down six categories under which data may be collected, entered or surveyed. These are goods, means of transport, companies, persons, tendencies in fraud practices and, ultimately, the availability of expertise. As far as the provisions on the operation and security of the CIS and on data protection are concerned, they are largely identical to the CIS Regulation. The CIS is a central database that can be used via terminals, and it is technically operated by the Commission. It is also important to know that information in the CIS database - and this is very important - can only be stored for as long as it can be used specifically for preventive measures, investigations and prosecution measures. After that they are to be erased. Personal data may only be recorded as necessary for the purpose of the CIS. National data rules and related provisions of the Convention of the Council of Europe of 28 Januarys shall be observed and continuously controlled by a joint supervisory authority, by data protection officer of the Member States. Ladies and gentlemen! If we make every effort to achieve pan-European security through the EUROPOL system, it is simply impossible to do so because economic crime is not involved. Security cannot be shared, security must cover the entire field of life. That is why my group will, of course, be happy to support this project in the sense of a comprehensive European security policy. - Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e15978	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The decision-making of the foreign package last summer was primarily intended to improve the legal status of foreigners legally resident in Austria. There have been changes and corrections necessary in the Foreign and Asylum Act, which in my view have very well solved the problems in practice in recent years. The principles of Austrian foreign policy are as follows: Integration before immigration, i.e. improved integration of foreigners already living in Austria. But illegal immigration must be avoided consistently. The foreign package is now in force for almost a year. The first experiences with the new law show the accuracy and success of these principles. It can therefore be said that the right of foreigners in Austria has largely proved itself. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Even with well-designed legislation, no 100 percent solutions can be found. With today's novel, such practical difficulties resulting from the implementation of the new foreign package are corrected. In the Foreign Act 1997 the issue of residence permits is reserved to the foreign authorities. The exhibition of the visual note takes place from abroad. The way of administration therefore goes back to the embassy, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the Ministry of the Interior, the state government to the responsible officers in the county headquarters and the same way. As a rule, such a procedure takes more than six weeks. In Austria, there is a temporary need for labour to cope with peak agricultural working hours, for example in vegetable production, cucumber harvest, strawberry harvest and so on. So it's about the use of the so-called seasonal eggs. However, since this labour demand can only be met in part by domestic workers, foreign workers are used. Farmers at that time depend on these harvest workers; otherwise, they are threatened with a loss of harvest. At present, as already mentioned, such procedures take a long time. What problems, then, are there? - On the one hand, despite many unemployed people, a rapid and flexible process is not possible to meet the corresponding labour needs in Austria; on the other hand, there is no need for the exhibition by the District Head Team as regards the aliens affected by it. The content of the coalitionary initiative proposal is therefore an independent exhibition of the view for special groups from abroad, provided that there is an employment permit. A residence permit can be issued for them directly by the Austrian Embassy abroad - but only in the case of sight notices up to a maximum and non-renewable duration of six months. This is an essential step towards administrative simplification and debureaucratisation. It is no longer necessary for a Czech, a Pole or a Slovenian, for example, to spend days and weeks waiting at the Embassy until finally his approval is issued. This takes into account the wishes of the Austrian economy, the Presidential Conference of the Chambers of Agriculture, but also the suggestions of science. This simplified and debureaucratised procedure is already being implemented by 1. It can therefore be used in a few weeks. That is why I would like to vote in favour of this draft law.

Since the start of the Federal Government Support Action for Bosnian War Refugees, six years have passed, and many of the approximately 90 000 to 93 000 Bosnian war refugees who came to Austria in these years have been integrated. Some 32,000 people have been granted residence and work permits. If we count this on family members, we can say that about 63,000 Bosnian war refugees have been successfully integrated into our country. 12 000 displaced persons have migrated to other countries, between 10,000 and 11 000 have largely returned to their homes with the support of the Austrian Federal Government. At the end of May of this year, about 5,100 Bosnian refugees were in the support campaign of the federal and state states. I believe that in one point of today's debate we are all in agreement, namely that this support action, which will take place at 31. In the past, a definitive new solution has now been adopted. And I believe that the present draft laws or amendments will bring this final solution. I assume that, under this new legislation, those displaced persons who have found work and accommodation in Austria and their families will have an unlimited right of residence. In the context of this system, we are also working together with the countries and the social sector to find appropriate employment opportunities for all Bosnia-Herzegovina not yet integrated over the next few weeks and months. These employment opportunities are to be found, in particular, for those belonging to the ethnic minority of the Republic of Srpska. That means, ladies and gentlemen, We have offered very many people in Austria help and reception. We have integrated very many people who have fled to Austria into our country, and many of these Bosnian war refugees have become not only integrated but also, in part, already Austrian citizens. For the rest of the 5 100, we have created a clear regulation with today's new law. In essence, these people can be divided into three groups. The first group are the around 850 Bosnians who come from the Federation. For these, a return home is possible, and we also want to implement this return by means of financial support, by means of return aid or through concrete projects in the coming weeks. The second group comprises the approximately 4 300 Bosnians from the Republic of Srpska. These persons are currently only difficult or only organized and can return with targeted, accompanying support from the host country. The third group consists of about 300 to a maximum of 500 persons; Persons who need permanent protection and are not likely to be able to return. These are not only people who are severely traumatized, but also people who are seriously ill, orphans and witnesses. For those people for whom no return is reasonable, this law should create a permanent right of residence and permanent right of residence. We also agree with the heads of state. It was also agreed with the Heads of State or Government that the costs incurred by these people would be covered by a joint action of the Federal Government and the Länder. There is also a joint agreement that will be agreed between the Federal Government and the countries in the coming weeks. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! However, I would also like to point out that we are not only doing what has been debated and demonstrated here today, but also that Austria has also participated in a number of return projects in recent years or is already supporting a large number of return projects and will also implement them in the coming weeks and months. The Bürgelkopf project is one of these that is carried out in the framework of vocational training, but also the Altersheim project in Travnik, and there are other projects in other cities as well. I believe that the Austrian Federal Government, the Austrian National Council and the nine Austrian federal states have contributed a great deal to the fact that the Bosnian refugees receive a corresponding support. The end of the right of residence and the end of the support action is therefore only for those people who can return to the Federation and for persons who can return to the so-called open cities due to special projects in individual areas of the Republic of Srpska. Furthermore, there will be an end to the right of residence for those people who, despite the possibility and offer of access to the labour market, have not accepted this offer or refuse to return without reason within the framework of a project. I believe that there must also be agreement that for those people who are not willing to take up work in Austria, neither are they willing to return, nor to benefit from the offer of further hiking, the action has ended, nor can the stay be extended. To sum up, my dear. Ladies and gentlemen: I believe that Austria has provided assistance and support to the Bosnian displaced to an international extent that is not comparable to any other country. I am convinced that Austria has given great support to the return aid both in the area of integration, financial support through donations or property services. In this sense, I believe, we can be proud of what we have done together. The current law serves to round off and end this action. I would therefore ask you to give your support to this draft law and to the amendment.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e18473	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The Heeresdisciplinary Act, whose revision is now to be discussed here, is also positively assessed by the Liberals in some respects. We also regard the reduction of the disciplinary commissions as a positive step, as well as the fact that, in the future, legal persons must act as chairperson of the Disciplinary Commission. We also consider it useful that the appointment of the Disciplinary Advocate and his alternate is made directly by the Federal Minister for Defence, and we acknowledge that there are some improvements for the accused as regards the limitation period or the introduction of an absolute penalty period, to the extent of three years. But it has already been working on us in the committee - and I have also noted this there and underlined it with an amendment - that in our opinion some provisions are in a tension relationship with the MRP. I have just had the opportunity to talk to Mr Maitz about it. He said that, after the discussions in the Committee, this had been resolved by the coalition parties. But we had no opportunity to exchange arguments. I therefore maintain our amendment and may briefly explain it in its essential points. On the one hand, it refers to § 5 para. 1 to be amended in Z 3 as follows: "In all cases where the essential aspect of a criminal offence has already been legally assessed by the criminal courts as well as the administrative authorities, the imposition of a disciplinary arrest is inadmissible." Ladies and gentlemen! They know that it is not a matter of being brought to trial twice for one case. I know - and this is what we have discussed in the Committee - that there is, in principle, a tension with disciplinary rights. In the case of a disciplinary arrest of no more than 21 days, such a tension ratio exists. We Liberals believe that it should not be on such borders developed in the Judicature when it comes to innovation, but we should decide in principle on this question. That is why we propose this amendment. In point two, the amendment states that § 73 para. 1 and paragraphs 2 to 7 become paragraphs 1 to 6. - The effect of this amendment would be that in future, as at present, it would no longer be possible to conduct the oral proceedings without the accused, if it seems that the facts would be clarifyable. In our opinion, we should keep the immediateity of the procedure very tight and not give any proof on this issue. This already existing provision would therefore be abolished. The third point of our amendment concerns § 83 para. 4, which should read as follows: "The defense of the accused during a service is only permitted by a defender of his choice." - Currently, it should only be permitted by a soldier. Ladies and gentlemen! We believe that there is also a tension with the MRP on this point, and the freedom to choose the defender should not be affected. As a whole, we believe that you too could help us to reduce the above-mentioned tensions with the MRP. We therefore cordially invite you to give your consent to our amendment. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e18593	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! With a view to formulating it in general, the novelle on the 1994 military disciplinary law has three objectives: simplification, cost reduction and efficiency, the second acceleration of the procedure and greater legal certainty, and the third, a clear distinction between disciplinary and criminal law. The drastic reduction in the number of Commissions from 19 to two in the future - as Mr Barmüller has already mentioned - allows cost reduction and efficiency to be reduced, but also offers the opportunity to achieve a uniform pronouncing practice in disciplinary conduct. The first instance will be in a Disciplinary Commission in the future, and there will be a Disciplinary Board in the second instance. The regional interests are made possible by setting up Senates in Vienna, Graz and Salzburg. The second area: The reduction of the limitation periods will speed up the procedures and can no longer be dragged away from both sides without end. Misbehaviour must be shown within six months at the latest, and the duration of the proceedings is now clearly defined by the three-year limit. Oral negotiations may also take place in the absence of the accused - but on a single condition that his presence for truth finding does not appear necessary. In this we differ from Mr Barmüller, for what is the meaning of this provision? - Procedures cannot be delayed infinitely by curious excuses, as we have seen them lately. That is the real reason why we want to carry out the procedure without hearing. [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Barmüller.]] The legal certainty of the accused serves as the new opportunity to appeal against the initiation of proceedings and against negotiating decisions. This proper appeal is - which is very essential - to be addressed to a non-profit-making collegiate body in the Federal Chancellery. The Chairperson of this Commission and his alternate shall be judges; therefore, this appeal authority shall be fully in line with the procedural guarantees provided for in Article 6 of the Convention on Human Rights. In order to clearly distinguish between disciplinary and criminal law within the meaning of the Convention on Human Rights, the extent of the possible detention and arrest penalty has been reduced from 21 to 14 days. It is particularly important to stress that these prison and arrest sentences may be imposed only in the event of serious offences under particularly difficult circumstances in the case of military operations. Such operations in this sense are, according to § 2 of the Wehrgesetz, a military intervention in the case of the state defense and assistance in the service of the Federal Ministry of the Interior, which is filled with sharp ammunition and is therefore a dangerous use. I repeat: There is no imprisonment and arrest in the peacekeeping and relief after natural disasters. We are also ahead of this in many Western European countries. The novelle 1998 to the Military Disciplinary Act therefore brings meaningful improvements to all parties involved and will receive our consent. I am now submitting the following amendment, which illustrates the procedure in case a criminal action can be committed at the same time as a breach of duty by court or administrative authorities: [[Quoted print version removed]] This makes it clear, and I am seeking the adoption of the bill, including the amendment proposal.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e18685	The amendment just read is properly submitted, duly supported and is therefore under negotiation. Mr Gaál has now been reported to speak. 5 minutes of voluntary time limit. - Please, Mr. Deputies.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e18703	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The draft law now before us and coming to its decision contains a number of basic administrative simplifications based on the practical experience of recent years. Organizational changes in the structure of the disciplinary organs are primarily normized. In the future, for example, there will be a drastic reduction in the number of disciplinary commissions, both in the first and second instances, and thus an increase in efficiency. There will only be a first instance disciplinary commission and a second instance, that is, a non-disciplinary committee - so far there have been five commissions in the appeal body. In addition, the chairmen of the Disciplinary Board and their alternates will be legal officials, which, as we have repeatedly called for, will ensure a quality improvement of the Commissions and more professionalism in disciplinary procedures. This will, of course, also generally improve the conditions for staff in the disciplinary procedure. Furthermore, the restructuring of the Commissions also improves proverbial practice. There will be a uniform federal jurisdiction throughout Austria. Through a variety of different measures - it has already been mentioned - the administration is simplified and the procedure streamlined; Circumstances that also bring financial savings. At the same time, a further objective of this legislative innovation, namely speeding up disciplinary procedures, is being achieved. Ladies and gentlemen! It is still worth mentioning a large number of measures that will improve the legal status of the accused, such as halving the period for the period of persecution from currently one year to six months, introducing an absolute period of criminality of three years from the initiation of proceedings, and a significant reduction in the maximum level of fines to be waived. The provision that, for example, the existence of gross defects, which are manifestly made apparent by a complaint, the abolition of the knowledge of discipline is only possible by the Federal Minister for Defence of the State, is also in the interest of the staff. Overall, this new army disciplinary law takes into account the intentions of the Civil Service Law. We therefore give our approval to this bill.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00129_d7e18823	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The military disciplinary law with the redefinition of the 1994 Army Disciplinary Act has undergone a comprehensive change. ==References== In January 1998, the Federal Army was open to voluntary military services of women, soldiers, necessary adaptations to military disciplinary rights. The practical experience has shown that a reduction in the number of Commissions, as has already been said by my previous speakers, was necessary. At the same time, it was mainly a question of simplifying the administration and improving the efficiency of these Commissions. The purpose of this amendment is to reduce these deviations from the disciplinary provisions of the Civil Service Service Law in 1979 in the sense of maximum harmonisation to an absolutely necessary level. Some tasks - such as the appointment decision on the opening and negotiating decision - will in future be carried out by the Board of Appeal established by the Federal Chancellor's Office in the same way as in the disciplinary law under the Civil Service Law 1979. This is for the discharge of the Administrative Court and the acceleration of disciplinary proceedings. In the future, disciplinary proceedings will only be interrupted if they are, at the same time, a judicial criminal act threatened with more than two years of imprisonment. This too is an essential point for speeding up the procedure. Similarly, the possibility of cooperation with the Public Prosecutor's Office at the stage of judicial preliminary surveys of the procedural economy serves. To date, for example, the Disciplinary Authority has not allowed any survey steps to be taken if criminal proceedings were pending. On the basis of pending court proceedings, there will be an end to disciplinary proceedings after the end of the interruption within half a year. Most of this innovation relates to the reorganisation of the Disciplinary Commission. - The reduction in the number of Commissions will increase quality and save considerable effort to date. In the future, the rightful withdrawal of soldiers in the presence shall be directly linked to an early release from the military presence. Since, in the case of a service, the person present is entitled to free accommodation and meals in the military At present, it is not taking into account the safeguard idea of the removal of services, which is the distance from the force. So this novelle certainly brings a substantial savings and administrative simplification with it, but - and this is also my heart - savings are constantly awakening fears among those concerned. I am particularly concerned about what happens to the individual disciplinarians, who are very familiar with this problem and do their thing very well. My question is therefore: How are these posts going? What will these disciplinarians do in the future? It seems to me to be important to talk to those concerned in good time to show them their future opportunities so that they are even more efficient and determined than before. We give our full support to the draft law or to these savings measures.
